ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e2715	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! It is of course the right of the National Council to discuss issues of interest here in plenary. And that is good, and as a newly elected member of the National Council, I would not be allowed to accept this right either. However, since the Rules of Procedure provide that, at a current hour, a member of the Austrian Federal Government has also participated in the debate from the government bank, I have agreed to do so, although, despite the most intense efforts, I have not been able to discover any competence on the subject of proporz and party book economy in the provisions governing the responsibility of the Minister of Finance. [[Cheerfulness at the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Scheibner: The Federal Chancellor did not want to participate!]] [[Abg. Dr. Martin Graf: There must even be President Fischer!]] I therefore want to make my contribution to the debate – and I understand this too – as a statement from the Government Bank, and I will try to ensure that you do not assume afterwards that it would have been wiser to speak from the Chamber of Deputies. This would not be in line with the Rules of Procedure. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Martin Graf.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! If you approach this issue – and I seriously believe it is an important issue – then you should look back a little on the history of our country. One of the basic thoughts in the establishment of the Second Republic was to involve all social forces in the process of reconstruction of our country. That was, of course, a completely different time than today. Never again – that was the thought that was expressed at the time – should Austrians face each other on barricades; Commonness, but also mutual control, was therefore in the foreground. These forces were essentially gathered – this is historically evident – in two major political camps, which, in relation to their strength, i.e. proportionally, should bear responsibility for the reconstruction of our democracy and its institutions. This was the thought, was the system called "proporz". Since then, I think it is important to note that Austria has developed into a very strong democracy, a country in which prosperity and peace prevail. Of course, the political framework has also changed in these fifty years. Today, there are several important political camps in Austria. The system of proportional involvement of forces in all institutions therefore no longer has the stabilizing function that can be derived from history. However, I would like to point out in all clarity that this cannot mean, on the other hand, that the political forces withdraw completely from the process of shaping society. I mean, it would be fatal, it would be wrong to believe that politically interested and committed people should be excluded from responsible tasks in the public service and in the public economy. This would make us a facilitator of a development which could lead to an opposite, yes, even dangerous situation for our democracy. Political commitment – I think every right here will give me – is the real foundation of any democracy. Politics and parties are therefore something valuable in themselves, because people who think politically, act politically and take responsibility for others also show their profile in this situation and carry out their tasks and their responsibilities. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Therefore, those who believe that politics and parties should no longer play a role should be of no importance, but this also implies that politics is not really worth anything. – I mean, anyway, that such an attitude is not correct. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] What would that mean? – We would be responsible for society and many important functions in this society – in the truest sense of the word! – let go of those who have no opinion and no attitude towards their work, people and society. But politics, as I mean, means responsibility, means willingness to design for society. And politics is also the design mission of society – legitimated by democratic elections. However, there is a need for politically interested people in all areas of society, and in the end there is a need for an instrument for implementation. We therefore need – to do so – people who are socially and politically interested and committed in the administration. We need officials who not only fulfill the letters of the law but also need those who are concerned about the impact of the laws on the citizens. I therefore say, of course, that the public service is looking for the best of minds, that the professional suitability in the case of occupations is examined in detail and is the prerequisite for inclusion in the public service. The tendering law has therefore also been valid since 1989, and there have since been calls for tenders and test procedures for all admissions. The Procurement Act has to enable information and access to all vacancies, it guarantees modern personnel selection procedures, and the recruitment is objectively achieved through verifiable procedures. I do not want to be apprehensive about the fact that there can be further improvements over and over again. That is why I also say a clear yes to the constant improvement of selection criteria. There are, for example, those proposals that SPÖ and ÖVP have drawn up for the purpose of targeting human resources policies in the event of new take-offs and promotions. For example, nationally anonymised candidate tests and performance determinations and greater control are proposed as well as ressort responsibility. That's what I'm saying. However, I say no – and I would like to point out in all clarity – to a bulwark of politics and a public service, in which political thinking and commitment of people would be a reason to be active or to move forward. I am of the opinion that it is not an advantage to be politically committed, but we also have to do everything we can to ensure that it will not be a disadvantage to be politically committed if we apply for a function. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] It is clear today that professional qualifications are the most important prerequisite for the occupation of functions in the federal administration or in public property undertakings. For public administration is on the public's critical test stand and has to prove and improve its performance constantly. Public ownership companies are also in an increasingly tougher international economic competition. It is therefore right to find suitable candidates for the freedom to provide services in public undertakings through calls for tender and staff consultants. Indeed, rules similar to those for the private sector should apply to public enterprises. This is why, in the previous year, we adopted the Employment Act, which sets out important provisions here. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Finally, I would like to make a few comments because they seem important to me, but I would like to add that they are personal considerations. No private owner, I believe, would ultimately have his manager's position occupied by a staff advisor. This is and remains the responsibility of the owner. He decides, for it is not the personal advisor who bears responsibility for the occupation or for any misdevelopments, but it is the owner who bears the responsibility. As in the economy, this should also be the case in the case of public undertakings or managers, as well as in the administration of: Exploration by staff advisors, but decision by those who also have the ownership function legally. I also believe that this is correct and indispensable in the sense of the exercise of responsibility. This is also true in other areas, for why, for example, were the new supervisory boards of KELAG not publicly issued in Carinthia, but simply ordered by Mr Landeshuptmann? [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: These are independents!]] I don't even criticize it. These are obviously personalities of his trust. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: Independent!]] I believe that it is important that professional qualifications play a role, but also that the production of the trust relationship with the person responsible is essential and correct. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I think the discussion is correct and good. I believe that it is wrong to become something in public administration or in public enterprises because of political commitment, but I am equally determined that, although there is a qualification, someone should not be in question only because he is politically involved. That would be a misdevelopment that I am not able to contribute.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e3300	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) The quality and performance of employees – I believe everyone will agree with me – are crucial factors for the success of companies and organisations, including political organisations and bodies. The staff decisions to be taken there are probably the most important management tasks to be carried out in these organisations. Only: The decisions taken by staff in these organisations have a completely different impact. In a private company which is in competition, which must claim to be on the market, personnel decisions are logical to contribute to the achievement of the goal. Finally, the quality of these personnel decisions is reflected in the assessment instrument available to companies moving on the market: namely, in the balance sheet of this company, which is to be assessed by the capital owner. We have experienced what the disdain of such criteria can cause in the national industry, which, although it must have been claimed on the market, has lost its share of 100 billion schilling by disregarding such principles. It was the ÖVP that enforced privatisation in this area as the only suitable instrument for the design of the decisions taken in these companies so that these companies could claim to be on the market. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! However, it is completely different in the whole area of public administration. There is no assessment instrument of the balance sheet, i.e. the incorruptible, impartial testimony to this sector. However, this means that in these institutions a spirit, a self-conception of competition, of result orientation cannot necessarily and automatically arise. This means that if personnel decisions are taken without taking into account these quality requirements, they do not automatically attract the "strave" of the market or the capital owner. – I know that this statement cannot be left out because it would be exaggerated and would mean a generalization also in relation to all those performance-oriented in these organizations, in this public administration. Because even the performance-oriented there suffers from this lack of distribution of evidence. What can the solution to this situation look like? – If an output assessment in the sense of the private sector is not possible, then you take advantage of the means, the input, i.e. the search and the selection of employees, to design differently. This is what we have done with the Employment Act and other instruments. There are new proposals – Khol and Kostelka – since these instruments are not yet sufficiently effective: for example, anonymized tests, independent control bodies, individual decisions of the ministers in their offices rather than the collective decision of the Council of Ministers. If the Minister is to stop his head for what is happening in his administration, he must also be able to take responsibility for the occupation on his own. What we need, however, is also a new philosophy of personnel management. She's not quite new: Instruments such as target agreements, employee talks and evaluations, competition between authorities or even performance-oriented payments have already been introduced or implemented, and they will help us achieve this. The first approach I mentioned, input instruments, is more a defensive one. The second, more competition, more power-consciousness and orientation in the administration, presupposes and causes a change of consciousness among the people there. Authorities in competition – and there are already examples of this, such as district captains who compete for shorter approval periods, and so on – also recruit their staff differently and in other respects. What we need throughout the public sector, ladies and gentlemen, is exactly this public debate that we are now holding...

ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e4596	We'll get to the 2nd. The item on the agenda and the debate is about to take place. It is known that, if there is no special limit on speaking time, each speaker can speak for a maximum of 20 minutes, but each can speak within the blockage period of his group. If individual Members wish that a shorter speaking time be set at the clock for their own information, it is customary to comply with this request. In these so-called voluntary speaking times, however, the red light does not mean the compelling end of the speaking time, but rather an orientation on the fact that the voluntary speaking time has expired. Of the ministers, none is present on the government bank, but I would like to welcome my colleagues to the Members' banks and just suggest, Mr President, that the votes, as stated in the Rules of Procedure, should be carried out in the future by the Chamber of Deputies and not on the government bank. As a vote this cannot be considered! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] To the referee law. I am very glad that a first reading of the Liberals' motion for a first reading is under way today, which has been initiated to deal with a truly unsustainable state. A few weeks ago, the Court of Auditors announced the adjustment factor, i.e. that percentage in the public sector, to which Members' contributions should be increased from next year onwards. It is 3.3 percent – a percentage that is probably unbearable in its level. Ladies and gentlemen! I really hope that we all agree on this. It is unbearable that politicians – no matter in which areas they are active – who share responsibility for the economic conditions, how the social community works, how the labour market develops, how the labour market is developing, this increase, on a proposal from the President of the Court of Auditors, but on a legal basis, the SPÖ and ÖVP here in the High House a few years ago – with great resistance to the liberals! – have decided to implement at a time when we will also discuss whether we will increase the pensioner 0.4 percent or perhaps 0.6 percent. So that means we're talking about whether a pensioner with an average pension of 10,000 s, who has worked for a lifetime to build this country, that we can live in prosperity and peace, now get 40 s more per month, or perhaps we're "biger"! – 60 S – gross! – and this at a time when the pensioners have just been taken away by savings packages and tax increases, not 10 or 20 s, but hundreds of schillings a month. [[Abg. Dr. Martin Graf: Gross!]] And then one should argue that for politicians, for example, with a salary of a national councillor of 100 000 S, there is not 40 or 60 S in increase as with pensioners, but more than 3,000 S per month. Ladies and gentlemen! I really hope – and believe it, because the discussions of recent times have shown it – that we all agree that this law, which was then adopted with the two-thirds majority of SPÖ and ÖVP here in the High House, is absolutely wrong, is contrary to the intentions of the reduction of privileges, is unfair and must now be corrected in the High House. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] I am very happy about this – and this is also the first result of this election result of the 3rd round. October – that there is no longer this two-thirds majority, that it is no longer possible for two government parties – no matter what the government will look like in the future – to adopt such laws with two-thirds majority here in the House. It has now been forced – even on the basis of this request of the liberals – to hold talks and to try to find an agreement, a two-thirds clarification here in the House for a new regime of political relations and adjustments. And I do not want to say here from the speaker's point of view that I am very glad that all four parties agreed in the talks on a regulation which, as I believe, represents a reasonable compromise. We will present this proposal today as a four-party proposal, and it will be the basis of the Committee's deliberations together with our proposal, which we have initiated and which is currently under discussion. I assume and hope that at the next National Council meeting in December this law, which is currently in force and which would have given us such an increase, will be corrected and that in the future we will push for it and ensure that there are no more real wage increases for politicians. This is the compromise: in the future, we are only about adjusting the salaries of politicians with the inflation rate, at most at the same time as the inflation rate. Ladies and gentlemen! It was a very important concern, especially of the liberals, for us to introduce a measure, a lid, namely the adjustment of pensions. If the changes in pensions are below this inflation rate in the future, then politicians' salaries will only be increased by this low adjustment factor. I hope that this too – but I would be sorry if such laws were needed – will in the future contribute to the fact that it can no longer be the case that pensions for the generation so important to us will be increased by a rate below the inflation rate. A first step towards this is this four-party proposal. Ladies and gentlemen! Another important freedom point was that the application did not immediately increase from 1. January, but at least half a year late, i.e. with 1. July next year. But – this should now be the occasion for further debates – there are a lot of other areas in the entire package of political privileges that are still unresolved. I would just like to address the following point: There was a reform of the references, and there was also a reform – which was an important concern of the liberals – of political pensions. But, ladies and gentlemen, I think it is incomprehensible that those who have already acquired rights here in the House, who are here long enough in the House and already have a right to a political board, then enter into this law: Those who already have the claim must not give up on it. Ladies and gentlemen! This is really a persil for the use of political privileges. It should be the next step that we should at least make this option possible for all honourable Members and Members who have been serving for a long time, so that they too, in the sense of a reduction in privileges and transparency of references to these claims, can fail. ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e6037	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Unfortunately, in recent weeks and months, the subject of foreign citizens in Austria, the issues of migration, asylum and immigration have again become a decisive issue in this Republic. However, I believe that this issue also gives the opportunity to work together to find new ways and make every effort to significantly improve the integration of foreign citizens in Austria. As has already been said, we currently have 750 000 foreign citizens in Austria. The vast majority of these people have come to Austria because they have hoped to find better economic or social conditions in that country. I believe that Austria has carried out a very successful migration policy in recent years and decades and that Austria has taken a right course. Of course, changes are always necessary, of course one must rethink the practice of execution, and of course one must be aware that the existing laws have gaps and weaknesses that need to be resolved together. I am convinced that the principles developed jointly by this Federal Government will continue to have their meaning and validity in the future. These principles are: yes, a human asylum policy, yes, to the best possible integration of our foreign citizens, yes to family integration, but also a no to a new immigration that would overstep the labour market and the housing market. I believe that with these principles a Minister of the Interior does not implement the policy of the Freedom Party in this country, nor does he automatically reject all that comes from the green or other side of it from proposals and ideas. My aim is to find a policy of principles, of clear principles, a policy that is exclusively committed to people – and here I say very consciously – of the indigenous and immigrants. The attacks that have been directed against me in the last few weeks and months are allegations and attacks I share with all the other Ministers of the Interior of Europe. Whether the Social Democratic Minister of the Interior, whether the Conservative Minister of the Interior, whether the Minister of the Interior, who is not a political party, is confronted with similar attacks and similar conflicts. I am thinking only of the discussion that is currently taking place in the Federal Republic of Germany, that the German Minister of the Interior, who has a completely different socialization and origin than I am, is exposed to a similar domestic policy debate as I am, or to the conservative Swiss Minister of the Interior, which is also in a similar situation. Ladies and gentlemen! Immigration must be clearly separated from asylum policy, and all of us, including me, all too often make the mistake of mixing these issues and problems. For me, it is also important to say clearly that, for economic reasons, immigrants in our country have had their right in the past, that we have also brought many people very consciously into our country, that we are also committed to these people, and that we must do everything we can to integrate these people in the best possible way in the interests of social stability in this country. But I also say very clearly, ladies and gentlemen, that this can only partly be the task of the Minister of the Interior, that all must be taken into account here, that this is the task of different ministries: The task of the Ministry of Social Affairs, the Ministry of Education, the Ministry of Family Affairs. The federal government has an important task here, but it also has a task for the countries, cities and communities. I could read to you a whole list of things that the Ministry of the Interior has set out in recent years on activities for integration. We spend a three-digit million each year on integration measures, and I believe that this amount will be necessary in the future. I believe, however, that we all have a common obligation to take more initiatives here. I therefore strongly oppose the fact that foreign citizens in our country are defamated or excluded. I believe that it is necessary and important to overcome all the difficulties of understanding and all the conflicts that exist between foreign citizens and the Austrian population. It is our common task to detemoration and overcome the difficulties that exist. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! But just as we must not negate fears, uncertainties, worries and, in part, hostile attitudes by Austrians and Austrians, but must deal very seriously with them. I am aware that we must do everything we can to combat xenophobia and racism. But I am also aware that there are real problems in the presence of a large number of foreign citizens and immigrants in our country. We must address these problems together. We must not put anyone in a corner, but our task is to solve these problems. But our task is also to stop making mistakes in the future, to link issues that have nothing to do with each other. It makes no sense to link the issue of crime and crime with immigration. It makes no sense to link the issue of the lack of housing exclusively with the issue of foreigners, and it also makes no sense to separate security for foreigners and foreigners. I believe that we should do everything we can to reject links and do everything we can to deal with these issues as they stand and how we are confronted with them. Ladies and gentlemen! As a Social Democrat, but also as Minister of the Interior, it is very important for me to avoid and stop any form of polarization and any form of exclusion from political dissidents. It must – this is our common commitment – not come to any further awakening of the mood in Austria. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I strongly oppose the call for "stop of alienation", but I also strongly oppose the call for open borders. And I think we should do everything we can to connect and not separate. That is why I invited the Ministry of the Interior for a first discussion next week to open an open debate on all the developments in this field; an open debate that discusses the weaknesses and strengths in the area of foreign rights in Austria, and which seeks to draw common consequences from it; a debate with all political parties, with all religious communities, with the most important institutions in that country, but also with officials who are assigned to the performance of the foreign sector in Austria and who are responsible for it here. I hope that this path, with a number of other measures that have been taken recently, will help overcome the division and polarization that has been created in our society in this area. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! In spite of this basic opinion, allow me also to say some specific things about the Greens' requests in this area which we are now present. I believe and acknowledge that we are very open to these requests. It would be a mistake to wipe out these requests and say that all that has been written here is a dangerous development, so we do not dispel. I offer you the open dialogue, together with the responsible officials of the Ministry of the Interior, on your proposals, on your concepts. However, I would also like to express my opinion on this because I believe that it is very important that we open up to each other. I also consider these proposals to be a fundamental preparedness for discussion of dangerous proposals, for proposals which, in combination, both in asylum and in foreign law, would in the future mean a new wave of immigration to Austria; a wave, as we had at the beginning of the 1990s, than more than 400 000 people in five years' time to Austria; and when we had very big problems in the integration we are still suffering today. I can't agree with the demand that a family quota should no longer exist in the future. I cannot identify myself with the requirement that the circle of family integration should be significantly expanded. I cannot say in solidarity that the declarations of commitment we have abolished in this country should be possible again, which has led to too much abuse, so we have abolished it. This has led to many dependencies that have also been used quite brutally. I cannot agree with your request that the controls on the legal residence of foreigners in Austria should be abolished. I cannot identify with your request that the ban on residence in the case of undeclared work should no longer be possible in the future. In general, the abolition of third-country security, as you call for, ladies and gentlemen, means that Austria will be the destination country, the number one of migration in Europe, means that Austria will in every form become the centre of asylum, but also immigration. A requirement as to how you put it, that it should be enough to say "Asyl" at the national border and thus get the right of residence in Austria, means that immigration is mixed with asylum policy, means that there is a magic word, namely "Asyl", and thus the gate to Austria is open. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that the general composition of your demands, both for changes in the asylum law and in foreign law, would contribute to the resumption of almost limitless immigration in Austria. I believe that although we discuss these demands very openly, we should be aware that, in sum, these demands would lead to an open border, to an open border of which the Greens have repeatedly claimed and stressed that they do not want them.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e6766	Minister! Mr President. High house! [[Abg. May. Schweitzer: Tell me my colleague Brinek didn't talk at all!]] I would now like to deal with the subject and also try to present factual arguments and to say that the requests made by the Greens are certainly not always understandable. But before I deal with the various points of the Greens' request for an amendment to the Asylum Act – because my colleague spoke about foreign law – I would like to say quite clearly that these proposals are not just an amendment to the Asylum Act, but rather a complete reversal of the current regulation. From this point of view, I would also like to look at these applications today. Now on the individual points of this request. The first point concerns the extension of third country security. The Asylum Act 1997 reworded third country security. It was then deliberately introduced the opposite approach. That's when it comes to whether the stranger in a third state was safe from persecution. We welcomed this regulation in its decision-making process, and we are still doing so today. However, the Greens' request also assumes that the third country security will only be valid if the third country has given the explicit consent that the asylum seekers can enter. Ladies and gentlemen of the Greens, are you aware of what that would mean? – This would mean that Austria would have to bear the main burden of asylum seekers because Austria is the first country to enter the European Union from the east. It can certainly not be that we have to bear the main burden of asylum seekers! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Let us look at the increases – the increases we already have – in asylum applications. In September 1998 we had 13 805 applications, in September 1999 already 15 261 asylum applications, so within a year already a tremendous increase. If we were to comply with the rules we wanted by the Greens, we would be expected to have an additional increase in asylum applications. This is certainly not acceptable to our country. Secondly, the re-establishment of the criteria for a clear justification: The current asylum law contains a provision on apparently unfounded asylum applications, and the Greens are now proposing a formula for when an asylum application is deemed to be manifestly justified. According to the Greens' proposal, for example, a homosexual tendency should be considered as a criterion of persecution and that such a request should be considered as manifestly justified. – This would mean that if someone in a state is persecuted because of his homosexual inclination, this application must be automatically given the right and that we have these asylum seekers here in Austria. I can't be sure that's what I'm not representing. [[Abg. May. Stoisits: Why not? But then you say why not!]] The third point concerns the extension of procedural provisions. The Greens' proposal is that a medical or psychological opinion should be sought in the event of doubts about the persecution. This proposal would mean a huge delay in the procedures and would also lead to an enormous cost increase. I reject that too. The next point is asylum at the border. The Greens demand a abolition of the possibility of rejection if the asylum seekers were already safe from persecution in another State. Even in the event of rejection in the already decisive case – if the asylum seekers have failed the refugee status – the Greens' proposal is that the rejection should be rejected and that entry should not be refused. These proposed measures would lead to a further increase in asylum applications. This is not acceptable from our point of view. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The fifth and last point concerns the Refugee Counsellors. Here, the Greens are leaving the support system for charitative organisations, which have carried out refugee advice so far and have undoubtedly done excellent work, and are demanding the appointment of their own refugee advisor. This is also a requirement that is not understandable to us. Coming to the end: The amendments requested by the Greens – I have already said this before – are a complete reversal of the current regulations. These changes would exorbitantly increase the number of asylum applications and would entail high additional costs. That is why, from my point of view, this proposal is not acceptable to our country, from the point of view of the EIA.

I ask you to: Let us work together to find a solution in this matter! It will only do well for universities and living together. Thank you.

Point First reading of Mr MMag's motion for a resolution 15/A. Dr. Madeleine Petrovicund Comrades concerning a Federal Law on the Protection of Animals (Federal Animal Protection Act – TschG)
ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e8156	We're on the 6th. Agenda item. Here, too, the first applicant, Dr Petrovic, first spoke. Voluntary speaking time limit: 8 minutes. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e8180	Mr President. High house! The Greens have submitted an application from the Austrian animal welfare movement as a request to the House and requested a first reading. The history is known – it is sad. In the spring of 1998 there was a very successful popular desire for animal welfare, which has also often been treated in Parliament, only: Until now, very little has come out. The basic idea of bringing animal welfare rights to the federal level was essentially shared by four political groups. On the part of the Austrian People's Party, there were concerns about this, and on the basis of the coalition agreement, the Social Democratic Party has remained in this position, so that animal welfare remains a national issue. I do not want to address in detail the individual sections of this very detailed legislative text, but it is important, as I have said, to highlight the following – there were also several preliminary drafts prepared by the Greens –: This draft stems from the Austrian animal welfare movement and has been examined several times by constitutional judges. It is therefore a draft that is very well thought out and carried and carried by all the essential Austrian animal welfare organisations, that is, hundreds of thousands of people. It is above all, ladies and gentlemen of the Austrian People's Party, a draft that tries to deal with the problem with a lot of sense of reality and with a lot of attention. I therefore believe that the arguments that have always been put forward are not at all sustainable. There was, on the one hand, the argument that the countries also needed some materials, especially since, after accession to the EU, national competence would be eroded anyway, and there would be other important materials, such as youth welfare, which are in good hands among the countries. This comparison is, of course, dramatic, because, of course, the standards for the protection of children and young people are also federal law where it is a question of preventing quails or neglecting them. Everything else – so that children or young people in the different federal states have a different level of protection – would be unimaginable. On the other hand, there is nothing to mention when it comes to funding for young people. This corresponds to an animal welfare promotion law, which the Greens have also proposed and which we believe is a legitimate task for the countries to take account of the very different circumstances as regards animal husbandry, especially agricultural animal husbandry. So this argument really doesn't draw. A second argument, which has always been put forward, is the objection that such a law would not make it possible to consider regional specificities. As far as free land is concerned, however, the weather and geographical conditions are very much affected. Of course! No reasonable person would oppose it! All of this is possible in a law that offers a wide scope for enforcement. But one thing has to be clear: The animals have been out of business in Austria since 1990. The Austrian legislator was progressive in Europe, when it was recognized that animals are sufferingable creatures and that full equality with lifeless things is not appropriate. That is why I do not think it is appropriate to subject the animals in the individual federal states, particularly as regards the rejection of quails of all kinds or any intervention such as the removal of horns in cattle and so on, to different regulations, because an animal does not feel otherwise, whether it is located in Burgenland, Lower Austria, Tyrol or Vorarlberg. It is factually justified to create a clear and clear level of protection. However, the promotion competence and all of this can, as has been said, remain with the countries. I have also heard no wish on the part of the federal states, which in principle support this concern with their liaison body. It seems that the countries do not speak the same language as the national leaders and the liaison body. I have never heard that this could not be a sensible regulation if, as I said, sufficient scope were created. In another argument, which I take very seriously, it has been said – and this is also partly seen by representatives of the Green Movement – that there could be a risk of reducing the already very good animal welfare laws in the countries by means of a federal law in their standards and implementing an adjustment downwards. Of course, we do not want that! That is why, I believe, it is very important for this draft to be in place. It is not an adjustment down, it is clearly about higher minimum standards. One last objection, which I do not make apply at all, was the argument that farmers would be threatened by it. To do this, I have to say: If there is an ally of farmers in Austria, it is the animal welfare movement. I would remind you that, in particular, it was the arguments of the animal protectionists – including the images that have gone through the media – who have made consumers more aware that animal protection is a value, that it is also important for product quality and that products produced in accordance with animal protection can, and must, achieve a higher price on the market. I believe that everyone from the animal welfare movement will be happy to take action to ensure that there are animal products from outdoor farming, and I would like to confirm that it is necessary to create transitional periods, to help farmers in the conversion and to remain flexible with the standards. That means: It should not now be prescribed a measure which, for example, is "run-off" or similar. We don't want that! We simply want to raise the standard together with the farmers as a whole, also in order to ensure the highest possible quality of production in Austria. – This is the very simple background of our request. I hope that in the near future I will have re-emphasized the essential counter-arguments from my point of view. I would be very pleased if the Austrian People's Party would reconsider its position on this point. I think you could also substantially lift the image of this House in public if we were to show: We have taken an important popular desire seriously, and we are willing to implement it here in this high House and give it legislative power.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e8460	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! We are now discussing at first reading a draft federal law on the protection of animals, which was adopted on 29 October. It was introduced by Mrs Petrovic in October 1999. It is in itself the same draft that was introduced in June. He was, as it were, almost taken over at the same time as text. In the first section of this bill, animal welfare is raised in the constitutional order, thus giving this legal matter a completely new position in the Austrian legal order. This would not be worrying in itself, however, one must already question the background. If, for example, the SPÖ and also the Greens have so far refused to raise the protection of the family, i.e. also those of children, in the constitutional rank, there is already a not entirely justified weighting of the issues. In the explanations it says – and that has made me stupid –: "As the constitutionally recognized co-creative dignity of all animals (co-creates) is inhabited (§ 2 para. 2), all animals also have a right to legal protection. A restriction of the scope of animal protection provisions on vertebrates or crustaceans, such as the current animal welfare laws of the Burgenland and Vorarlberg – that is, the countries – "is therefore not to be justified." But somewhere, ladies and gentlemen, a border has to be drawn. I know from the media a case of Carinthia, in which it is said that a gardener who has used a snail death has been reported because of animal torture. Yes, according to this definition or according to these explanations, can there be no further harm to a fly? In this regard, more detailed explanations are necessary. The present draft establishes the co-financing of animal welfare from public resources. If there are precepts which, for example, require conversions in the stable systems to animal-friendly behaviour or to the required animal-friendly attitude, then these measures should be supported by public means as well. On this point I may call an unwarranted witness. There has already been an application from the National Council to explain how the stable conditions should look. There are national laws in which the animal justice index is already included. On the basis of this information, the University of Soil Culture has prepared a study that would only cost 34 billion schillings for the conversion into cattle farming. This can simply not be achieved by farmers alone in current income conditions. There is also a new EU Bio Directive. This Directive requires free movement of goods. On the other hand, there was a uprising of the small organic farmers who all had to change their stables to free run. A compromise was therefore also reached, so that up to a stock of 15 cows, organic farmers can also be allowed to use an attachment device. In a runway stable, a milking position must be set up, and thus would be disproportionately high in comparison to the low yield of a small animal husbandry. We have 20 cattle in Austria on average, of which eight cows. In the case of eight cows, it is not possible to establish a milking position which would never be expected unless the additional costs resulting from it could be borne by public authorities. But I doubt in the given budget situation, which will also be in the house in the next few years, that this additional money will be raised. This draft, which I must mention already, and also towards the Freedom Party, is spoken differently at the farmers' assemblies than here, already brings a huge bureaucracy. § 34 states: "Farms in which agricultural animals are kept..." – that is, only in the case of farmed animals, not in the case of pet animals – "are to be checked by the Authority at regular intervals, at least once a year, regardless of the existence of a reasonable suspicion...." - Quote. I think that we need to be recognized in this respect, because that will also cost the authorities. There are about 180 000 animal farmers in Austria. If these 180 000 farms need to be reviewed and monitored at least once a year, including without suspicion of poor animal husbandry, then there will be considerable costs. I see this as rather a shock to the farms. Such short-term checks are not even required in the worker protection provisions of the farms as they would be prescribed here for agriculture. [[Abg. Aumayr: But the agricultural market in Austria can already do it?]] The agricultural market Austria controls 5 percent of farms, but not 100 percent every year at least once, as is proposed here. Very many animal husbandry regulations are only governed by regulations which are not yet in place in the draft. In the regulation – I take the original, otherwise you would say I have read it incorrectly – it is stated in § 12 para. 2 verbatim: "In the Regulation, in accordance with § 10 (Animal Regulation) has the Federal Minister for Women's Affairs and Consumer Protection" – listen now! – "in agreement with the Federal Minister for Women's Affairs and Consumer Protection." So not the Ministry of Economic Affairs or the Ministry of Agriculture, but twice only the Federal Ministry for Women's Affairs and Consumer Protection is responsible in this area! And here, Mrs Minister or now Mrs Prammer, we are children who have been burned because of the hormone accusation we have experienced last year. If, so to speak, you can create all the regulations yourself without the competence of another ministry, we are already very skeptical. It also refers to the transitional provisions in this draft, in accordance with § 51: All existing stables must be controlled and approved within one year of the adoption of this law. If the provisions are not complied with, the animal husbandry must be neglected to the farmer concerned. Ladies and gentlemen! The resulting conversion costs of 34 billion schilling, as determined by the opinion of the School of Soil Culture. It also says: In addition, each animal holding is subject to authorisation, with an opinion of the official veterinarian still to be obtained prior to authorisation. – We see this as very bureaucratic in your conception. Ladies and gentlemen! The income of the Austrian farmers consists of one third of the proceeds from plant production and two thirds from the proceeds from animal finishing. It is a matter of course: Of course, there are black sheep everywhere, you can't rule out that. But in general, the farmers treat their animals in good time, only in our small and medium-sized farms, where the farmers even have their own name for each animal. We farmers in Austria are committed to higher environmental protection, we are also committed to higher animal protection, but in a single market in Europe we are bound by borders. In the WTO negotiations, this must also be done with a negotiating object that countries that accept and implement high animal welfare standards will then not be exposed to world market prices with their products. Again, I would like to say: The Austrian farmers, apart from a few exceptions that exist everywhere, treat their animals very pleasantly and gentlely. They also know that economic success also depends on the performance of the animals. These will have better performance if well fed and maintained.

The sense that the Austrian Federal Government has drawn up a very clearly defined, open, fair anti-atom plan, decided in the Council of Ministers and also informed you, was that we are bringing this plan to the attention of all the Member States of the European Union, all partners in good time, but also of all the candidate countries, whether they are already negotiating or not. The Austrian position – which we have always considered important – is not to threaten, but it is to inform our partners in good time about the clear attitude of our country, which the population expects from us. In this form, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the embassies, and also every member of the government have already sent this information to the candidate countries. In the course of the traditional tour of the Capitales, I will also have the opportunity to explain personally to the current President-in-Office of the Council, Paavo Lipponen, on the occasion of his visit here in Vienna. Our basic attitude and our basic objective are clear: We want to achieve an earlier closure of Bohunice V1. However, we are also aware that a state like Slovakia will need our support and that of the whole Union. Negotiations with the Finnish Presidency and the Commission have already begun. Question 8: They believe that the Anti-Atom Action Plan only states that "the existence of comprehensive and convincing closure plans is the basis for accession-related decisions in Helsinki", and so on. Ladies and gentlemen! We are ready to support Slovakia and we know that the European partners must support all these countries. Schweitzer: When?]] I assume that Austria will also make its contribution to this and that we will receive broad approval from the National Council. They would have been attacked in Slovakia anyway. It is only about the cost of early closure. I think you are right in this regard that these 1.5 billion schillings, which are called shillings, are a number that still need very careful analysis and estimation. ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e11263	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I do not know if it is good to hold this debate today. But I think that's a point that we shouldn't be emotionalizing here so that there's going to be grounded fronts again. [[Abg. Dr. Martin Graf: Many of the Social Democrats are not for this!]] – Mr Colleague! I can only tell you: There is a corresponding vote, and you know that. In the end, they have also helped us to do so, on the basis of our previous and final voting behaviour. I think it is necessary that we continue this discussion, which is now being held, because there is no rational argument anymore for maintaining the state we are currently in and which is now unique in Europe as a whole. Mrs Lunacek! You have referred to it: In the context of the European bodies, there have been numerous decisions, instructions and calls for proposals, which clearly state that our understanding on this issue is a completely unsustainable one and cannot be reconciled with human dignity and human rights. There has also been extensive work on the subject, and I have the feeling that things are moving, that we are moving forward in the discussion. That is why I say again: I think it is good to have this discussion in such a way that, after the discussion, one can really talk more and not dig into rigid positions. This discussion has also set out material aspects. Last but not least, in 1995 an expert hearing within the Committee on Justice made it quite clear that the decision-making bases, which the Constitutional Court has also set out in its deliberations on this matter under § 209, are no longer to be maintained. What I address is the so-called embossing theory. For a long time, it was argued that young men, faced with a similar sexual behavior, are shaped in this direction for the future and are therefore more protective than girls and women, for example. In the context of this discussion there was a presentation of scientific findings. I think you can't simply push these insights aside, even if you say you're not emotionally yet able to really deal with it and accept it – which I also understand. But I mean, you can't completely ignore on the other hand that it was shown that this embossing theory is incorrect. There is now no more serious voice across Europe that continues to maintain this theory. But this is the main point of the argument, why § 209 should actually be maintained. It is present in such a way that in Austria we distinguish between men and women in terms of sexuality and protection, without any material basis, because the above-mentioned factual argumentation is not correct. This situation is not to be maintained, and I should therefore ask to continue the discussion in this sense. I really distance myself from every finer word message and invite you all to further conversations. I think we really owe it to ourselves – it's about human rights! – to further develop these things. - That's the first point. The second point – you mentioned it, Mrs Lunacek – is the community of life. I mean, two things to say. This is not only about sexual societies, but also about heterosexual communities. What is the relationship between society and those who, for example, cannot decide to marry for formal reasons, but who still want to live together? Should it really be so that we say the only form of formalized living is marriage? Or should we say that we cannot prescribe everything, there must also be other forms, such as the registered community? We are for the registered community, which you can register for example at the office or elsewhere – you can talk about formalism – and explain to the two people to want to live together. This concerns both heterosexuals and homosexuals. There is simply a need in this country to live together without marriage, with, of course, lower legal consequences. But it is not understandable for me, for example, that if two people who have lived together for 25 years left one dead and no will, one assumes that the legal succession is the one that is currently planned – that is, under the exclusion of the partner, although it is clear that, due to the duration of this community of life, the person who died would certainly have preferred his partner. I think you should be able to offer such a thing. It doesn't have to be accepted. The fact is that there are not a few people – and there are getting more and more; especially with the heterosexuals – who say we want such a community of life. I think it is hypocritical to say that we do not really accept what is asked, do not really discuss and, ultimately, do not offer. Everyone is free to choose the one or the other within the limits of their power. I believe that this would not be an evaluation of marriage – this argument almost always comes in this context – but an alternative that has a different regulatory framework from the consequences. I think that's just necessary. Now to the individual points: We have introduced the testimonies, the right to give evidence in the criminal procedure, even for the same sex communities. The consequence of such a renunciation is that the person who has been living with another or another for a long time must not be reasonable that he says against his partner. However, there is no justification at all for the fact that what was introduced by us in the Criminal Procedure Order does not have to be introduced in the Civil Procedure Order, because there is no difference in content, whether the witness is involved in criminal proceedings or in civil proceedings, since the consequence is the same. This is something that is completely undisputed among professionals, namely that this divergent is not justified in fact. I am therefore saying: If we want to move here in the context of a more objective discussion, we either have to abolish one or equal the other. However, we are ridiculous if we continue to maintain this difference. That is why I would ask you to discuss this subject-matter as well. We are sure to conclude that what is currently decided is not optimal and, in fact, legally dogmatically non-sustainable. To the rental law: With the provision that this community should be a registered one – in order to offer here a form that makes it comprehensible, so that it does not dispute what it actually was – we are for that. I have already said something about social security and legal succession. All of these are points that we wanted to discuss further, with which we should take decisions, which – I say again – are about human rights, and we should therefore also discuss this issue accordingly and then take appropriate decisions. - Thank you.

If the state is so generous and if possible many give the right to witness, up to psychologists and psychotherapists, including life-mates, whether gender-oriented or not, this is his case, but in the civil process the material truth needs to be researched, and therefore I am not for a limitless privilege. A state objective for the respect, preservation, promotion and protection of the linguistic and cultural diversity of the Republic of Austria is something that has been discussed several times in this House, also during a first reading in the last legislative period, but not only there, but also in interesting – let us say this – events of the Constitutional Committee or at the invitation of the President in cooperation with the national councils and the representatives of the Austrian autochthonian minorities. I always had the impression – if I let this happen in the memory of Revue, I can confirm it – that it has given a very high degree of consent to this objective, namely to give a commitment to cultural diversity in the sense of respecting, preserving and protecting it. In the SPÖ, in the ÖVP, even with the liberals, there were positive statements about this intention – let us say it so. Why there has been no decision-making in this regard in the past legislative period is once again the subject of higher political mathematics, for apples have been mixed with pears or unauthorisedly linked. The cultural agreement with Slovenia has something to do with the advisory committees for the Styrian Slovenians, and this has to do with the fact that it is not the respect, preservation and promotion of linguistic and cultural diversity in relation to it. Ladies and gentlemen! Nothing of that would have been understandable to the Austrian citizen or the Austrian citizen. The fact is, nothing has happened in this direction. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, my initiative at the beginning of the new legislative period, with the word-identical repetition of this request, here is a sign that the implementation of this issue of importance has not failed at all. In this context, I would just like to say one word: Chief. The key to awareness-raising were the murders of Oberwart and the tragic events during the period of post-bombing in Austria. At that time, the verbal willingness to take seriously the respect, preservation and promotion of cultural diversity, as our proposal now means, was because it often resulted, I say, or almost exclusively from deep concern. In the meantime, a few years have passed, and in the meantime, there has also been some change in the proverbial climate – this has nothing to do with the Federal Chancellor – in this Republic. Now the feelings seem to be spread differently. Ladies and gentlemen! At first reading, we want to give the political groups the opportunity to give them their commitment to respect, preservation and promotion and to protect the linguistic and cultural diversity of the Republic of Austria, and at first reading we want to give them the opportunity to take this step. Ladies and gentlemen! To define multilingualism as a state goal is nothing new. Switzerland has long done this. There are other European countries, however, which, as far as the democratic standard is concerned, are in no way in keeping with Austria. On the contrary! In part, they have already taken such steps. In Switzerland, for example, the basis for this was a positive referendum. At the time, the reason was that Switzerland sees itself as a state of solidarity, so to speak, as a state of self-understanding, and therefore considers four languages in its territory to be self-understanding. Well-marked: four languages! That is – of course, one could say – German, of course French, and also of course – although that does not fit into this series from its number relationship at all – Italian. But even the fourth language, the Romanian, is treated simply and simply the same, no different from the others. I should like to say this again to you, ladies and gentlemen, that is my concern today, and I am already looking forward to the statements made by the other groups. Because here's something that nobody threatens, that doesn't take anything away. It is the opportunity to express that the protection of minorities, by bringing them away from the periphery into the middle, is the way Austria wants to go in connection with its autochthonic minorities.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e12125	The next speaker is Mr Magistrate. Posch. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e12146	Mr President. High house! Mrs Stoisits has already said this: We have already held the debate on the state target in the last legislative period several times. Even if this is more a symbolic act than a factual aid and support, it would still be important. The SPÖ has already made a proposal in this regard, according to which the Republic of Austria has become itself by creating an abstract. 2 to Article 8 of the Federal Constitution on their groups and the ethnic, cultural and linguistic diversity resulting from their existence – rather than constitutional protection by the State Basic Law. So we don't have any dissent here at all. It would be the Republic's first constitutional commitment to its ethnic groups, since such a confession by the Republic of Austria has so far been lacking. Confessing the confession is one thing to do something is the other thing. In fact, much has happened in the past to protect minorities. In the last legislative period, the Framework Convention on the Protection of National Minorities has been ratified as the first legally binding multilateral instrument dedicated specifically to the protection of minorities and which provides for reporting on the situation of minorities and also the succession of national legislators. It is a proven instrument for popular promotion as a fact. It was still at 4.4 million schilling in 1988 and now amounts to just over 50 million schilling, while the impact of the financial nature of the minority school law has not yet been taken into account in this number. It has been passed in the last legislative period the Private Radio Act, the two minority-language radios, which are very essential or even indispensable for the development and transfer of language competence, namely radio "Agora-Korotan" and the Burgenland Radio "Mora". So a lot has happened. Nevertheless, there is still much to do. In addition to the above-mentioned State objective, the overall Austrian conference of the chairmen of the People's Advisory Councils should be yanked. Unfortunately, such a conference has failed in the last legislative period, partly also on the dissent of the ethnic groups themselves. It once took place on 8 May. In April 1997, such a conference was held with an encouraging objective and with an encouraging beginning. Unfortunately, this has not been found yet. Unfortunately, the European Charter on the Protection of Regional and Minority Languages has not yet been ratified. You can say that, too, self-critically. There are no longer many members of indigenous minorities in Austria. Nevertheless, it is significant how a state deals with its minorities, whether it be autonomous minorities, or new minorities within the framework of migration movements in Europe. There are many encouraging but also many depressing signs. Mrs Stoisits has said that the trigger for raising awareness of this problem was actually the attack of the Lord. Although a single perpetrator was at work here, the question of how the social environment must be created must be allowed to thrive. Therefore, the SPÖ is also a gesture towards minorities for this objective. Although it does not cover the entire minority protection, it is a gesture, and we very much hope that it will be possible to enforce it during this legislative period.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e12189	The next speaker is Mr Dr. Ofner. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e12210	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, I can turn to Resi Stoisits. - Where is she hiding? Resi! I have the impression that if I listen to you in this way – and with others I have had similar feelings – you are sorry that the perpetrator has really been caught in matters of upper guard and letter bombs. [[Abg. Scheibner: Yes, that's what it looks like!]] [[Abg. May. Stoisits: How do you get such an abstract idea?]] That doesn't fit into the system you built yourself! You live as if you hadn't read newspaper in recent years and didn't hear radio either: It's still the great conspiracy, there's still the Bajuvarian battalions marching there. That this was a spidered single, that doesn't fit in completely! Because for years you – I've been putting that under you, because I've been watching this – have felt you connected to a ghost behind which all possible dark powers are behind which you need to warn and protect the population. But now this is a "poor" – poor under quotation marks – fool, who was alone and was sentenced. Please free yourself from the ghost you've woven yourself into in recent years! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Now I turn to my immediate previous speaker – it's interesting: when the same topic was discussed many months ago, you also talked to me, and the Resi was before us, I think it was about the same order –: I used to talk about "minorities." "minority" is a mathematical term – it is noted in this context. Of course, groups of people are less in mind than the majority population, but the term "minority" has something diminutive in itself. I'd rather use the term "people's group". I don't want to prescribe anything, but I think the people concerned are better off seeing it as "people groups" and not using the expression "minority", which – perhaps also from the past – has something horrendous in itself, for them. The initiative on and for itself is good. It finds the basic consent of the liberals. We have already given everyone together the last time, one after the other, from this speaker's desk to the very best that we assume that this project will find the way into the federal law paper, even if one may have to do so. In principle, however, such a thing is good and should actually be realized. When it comes to what we liberals wish for, I could imagine that we are more clearly talking about the indigenous groups in their native homeland, so that we are not making things so commonplace that everything can be rebuilt again in the future, but that we are thinking of the groups in the political and legal sense we are experiencing in Austria. These indigenous peoples are to find a corresponding anchorage in the Constitution in their native homeland with their rights and with our wish – and promise to work on it – that they will flourish and thrive. In recent years, we have achieved approaches in this direction – in my view, too little. There are groups of people who are good, but there are also groups of people who are not well. The Roma and Sinti are among those who are not well. That's not new either – not from this speaker's mouth or from my mouth. They have a catchup requirement that counts after centuries. They not only have the usual national political problems, they also have a serious social and economic need. Only those who have looked at things on the spot, such as in the surroundings of Oberwart in the respective settlements, who have been in direct contact with and stand, can judge that. There are groups of people living. There are groups of people who are worse, such as the Czechs, who have problems with the Comensky School. There are also those whose reality is not consistent with all the standards we have used to in terms of life and the needs of the peoples of Austria, and to that - I repeat it - the Roma and Sinti belong. We're going to have to get up and see that we're going a little bit further – budget scarcity, savings package. It is also about putting this people's group and their families into a situation that brings more acceptance in their immediate local and human environment. There is no second ethnic group in Austria, which has to fight so much to be treated with dignity as is the case with the Roma and Sinti in their native homelands. If we confess to it – and we all confess to it – that the groups of peoples in Austria should flourish and thrive if we try to help them accordingly – not only with words but also with deeds – then we want to not forget, as I hope everyone else, that this is not a problem that will stop at the borders of the state. It is certainly true that, for example, the Hungarian people's group in Austria should have its sufficient level of rights, but in our view things are also so that the ethnic group of the Altösterreich German tongue should find an appropriate level of rights beyond the border in Hungary. This is not so dramatic in Hungary. There, things on both sides of the border function more or less enough. But there are also other areas in which this is by no means the case. I recall, for example, that there are still 60 000 to 100 000 in the Czech Republic, depending on the number of posts, or, in the opinion of some experts, even 200 000 Old Austrian German tongues, with zero rights, with zero schools, with zero kindergartens, with zero recognition of their language before any authorities, with zero recognition as a ethnic group. It is not much different in Slovakia and not much better, although a little better. It is very bad in Slovenia: There is also the curiousness that, when the new constitution was created after the turn, there was a very balanced concept, a very reasonable draft. In this draft, as groups of people who are recognized and promoted and to be preserved, the Italians, the Hungarians, the Altösterreich German tongues and – which is already surprising – the Roma and Sinti have found themselves with a little less rights. Sometime on the way through the legislation, however, the Altösterreicher German tongue fell out, and only the others remained. What arguments the Slovenian government is now trying to argue with are hardy. For example, that one says that the minority can only look more at the cemeteries and similar, testifies to a cynicism, if one recalls how one has been dealt with these old Austrians at the time. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] We would like, ladies and gentlemen, or could we imagine – this idea would have to take the form of concrete wishes – that we should set up advisory councils in the medium term, each accepting the problems of the ethnic groups in this respect and beyond a certain border. For example, such a advisory board would be concerned with the problems and rights of the Slovenian People's Group in Austria, and the same advisory board in the same occupation, and therefore, I assume, with the same requirements, would perceive the problems and rights of the ethnic group of the Old Austrian German tongue in Slovenia. For the other borders with the other states, this could work equally so that a balance can be established in this sector, because also popular law cannot be or remain a one-way road. We Austrians are proud that we are very positive about our groups that we are trying to make them thrive in what they need. In the long run, however, we cannot see that this is not a response to our process from beyond the border. We also wish – and I call on those responsible, the Chancellor and, above all, the Foreign Minister – to work on it – that at last progress is made in the design of a European People's Group right, which ensures a minimum level of rights for the groups of indigenous people in all European countries. It must be a enforceable law – not a patch of paper – which ensures a certain minimum level. It must not be like the French who say: There are no groups of people with us, there are no members of the groups, there are only French. This is, as it were, the lowest level. This right must in fact create a uniform minimum level, and it must be enforceable accordingly. There are so many other things on the European parquet that can easily be enforced. The rights of the ethnic groups should not be excluded here. These are thoughts that come to you when it comes once again to the issue of state objectives in relation to the ethnic groups in Austria. We liberals – I repeat – want this path to continue. We hope that it will succeed in achieving a positive conclusion. Our hand should not be missing in this context.
