It is a coincidence that I am able to hold my second virgin speech here in Parliament on a subject that is always at my heart personally, not as a theorist, but rather as a practitioner of the People's Group Policy in my role as head of state of Carinthia. Ladies and gentlemen! I have listened very well to Mr Stoisits, who I would also like to say a very friendly ‘Dobar vecer’, and I believe that if one is so simple, there would actually be no reason to have long discussions on the subject. If this proposal states that the Republic of Austria confesses itself to its growing linguistic and cultural diversity, this diversity must be respected, preserved, promoted and protected, then it is not surprising if there is great harmony in this House, for who could vote against such a request? Ladies and gentlemen! I mean, however, that we need to discuss this issue with a lot of seriousness and seriosity, too. That is why, Mr Dr. Ofner, I believe that although it is quite legitimate and legitimate, the discussion may not be appropriate if one makes a completely autonomous decision by the Austrian National Council, at least in one speech, depending on decisions in our neighbourhood in other countries. [[Abg. Dr. Ofner: You misunderstand me! I may have been misinterpreted. Not dependent, but autonomous, but it can't be a one-way road in the long run!]] - Okay. I think this is a decision we have to make here. I believe that this applies to a country that already has to take note that this growing diversity is an essential part of the identity of this Austria and that this diversity is also an unmistakable element of the Austrian nation, something we should always be aware of. I believe that this harmony is also justified because this commitment is also reflected in the daily active and practical community policy in our country. Ladies and gentlemen! The fact that we still have to discuss very serious and serious is that a state objective is a positive definition of our federal constitution, which ultimately also has a binding effect and which will have very concrete effects on both legal projects and execution. Here, ladies and gentlemen, we should not forget to point out that, I hope, we have the same starting position, namely that, as the target groups of this provision, we see those indigenous groups in Austria, which actually exist as growing and not growing minorities in our country. We know that this definition, if it is done in this form, affects around 60 000 citizens in our country, according to the People's Group Report, but that in Austria we are sure to record the tenfold number of citizens of foreign minorities. However, I would like to prevent this discussion from being mixed and mixed with discussions on immigration policy. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, I think it is important and necessary to ask very serious questions in this context in the Committee, such as the following – and our third President, who has so far been working on this subject accordingly from our side, will also be prepared to discuss them very seriously –: What groups of people should in fact be covered by such a provision? Should emerging minorities also be covered? – For it is now the case under international law that a group of people is defined as an autochthon after about three generations and can thus arise. Are there organizational or financial needs that arise in addition from such a state objective? Should the countries most likely to be affected in this context also be included in this discussion? And much more would have to be considered. I think that this makes the topic a little more differentiated in space and it is not to be qualified as a no-na! question. In any case, we will have such a discussion on our part with all seriousness and seriousness, and, of course, we will be aware that all of us here in this House are surely in agreement with the basic need underlying this request. - Thank you many times.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e12341	The next speaker is Mrs Pepper. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-11-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00002_d7e12362	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! I can already support the basic direction of this request for amendment of Article 8 of the Federal Constitutional Act insofar as the SPÖ, as is generally known, had made a similar request during the previous legislative period. This request was also presented to the public during the previous legislative period, but was no longer submitted for known reasons. According to our proposal, the new wording would read as follows: The Republic of Austria acknowledges its peoples and the linguistic and cultural diversity resulting from their existence. – I think it would be quite appropriate at the threshold to the next millennium that the Republic of Austria, in this explicit form, confess itself in a central part of the federal constitution to its peoples. [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Brosz.]] On official occasions and celebrations, representatives of the Republic often emphasize the importance of the Austrian ethnic groups for the identity of our state. Many organisations of the ethnic groups have also called for a corresponding government target in recent years. For example, the Memorandum of Understanding of the Austrian People's Groups, drawn up by representative representatives of all Austrian groups, called for the adoption in 1997 of a constitutional objective, which is to include a commitment by the Republic of Austria to its historical cultural, linguistic and ethnic diversity. I think it is important that the word "people's groups" be explicitly used in such a formulation, as this term is clearly defined in the People's Group Law. To that end, I think that the Greens' request, as I have said, is good and correct from the basic point of view, but not in its concrete wording. However, we do not need to talk in detail here in the first reading, but this is reserved for the Committee. As a citizen, I think that the peaceful living together of various linguistic groups and groups is very important. In addition to the majority of the German-speaking population in Burgenland, we have also accommodated the Croatian and Hungarian groups as well as the Roma ethnic group. Even if something can still be improved, you can generally say that living together with us in the Burgenland works well. But if we look beyond the borders and make events of recent years happen in Europe, we see how much unhappiness and suffering can cause the spirit of nationalism and intolerance. That is why I am in favour of us in Austria pursuing a modern ethnic group policy, which should be a conscious counterpoint to the transformation of various forms of nationalism. We must be aware that our peoples are an enrichment of our society and that it is important to maintain these groups. For me, it is important that interethnic contacts and projects be promoted and that the individual groups are not separated from the majority population but deliberately integrated into society. A real co-operation is necessary and not a mutual one, but also not a conciliatory one. In this sense, my party has long called for an improvement in the representation and expression possibilities of the ethnic groups, in particular the upgrading of the national councils by extending their competences and improving their functioning. For as praiseworthy as it may be to introduce into the Constitution a state target for the ethnic groups, so it would be also important to develop the People's Group Law in concrete terms. Although this law has proved itself in principle since 1975, it could be adapted in some respects to the requirements of today's times. As mentioned above, it seems to me particularly necessary to upgrade the national councils. Because the action often heard by the representatives of the ethnic groups is that decisions of the national councils are not taken seriously enough by the addressees. In the last legislative period, there have already been proposals from our side, how this could be resolved and how the decisions of the national councils could be given a higher weight. Another weakness of the existing People's Group Act is that it contains provisions that are only conditionally suitable for the Roma's People's Group. Thus, the composition of the appropriate national advisory council of an emergency construction had to be used. Finally, I would like to express the hope that in the next legislative period, it will be possible to make progress in the legislation for the groups. When we set accents on a modern group policy, we also set signs against nationalism and a democratic, tolerant and humane society.
